### War between realms and PvP (GUE)

- [x] **GUE-02 Shared catalog of realms and sides** · F2 · essential · S · shared — A catalog in `scripts/shared/war/` with the three realms and the neutral side of the Hollow. For each: id, network code (0 neutral, 1-3 as NetProtocol.Realm), name in Spanish, side color (#7EC8E8, #E9C341, #C3242F, #BDB6A6) and emblem. It also includes the realms admitted per zone that NetZones requires (vado_gris: vardheim and solmarch). Finished when Zones, NetZones and the banners read from here and a test checks the id↔code conversion in both directions. Depends on: MUN-01, TEC-04.
- [x] **GUE-03 Hostility rules, per-zone PvP and safe zones** · F3 · essential · M · shared, server, content — Shared `FactionRules` with `can_damage(attacker, target, zone)`: no friendly fire, the Hollow are hostile to everyone and enemy realms only damage each other in RvR zones. Each zone in the registry carries its PvP mode (SAFE in capitals and PvE zones, RVR in Vado Gris and Eldrun). `safe_areas` volumes with `realm` metadata at each Vado Gris camp (radius 24 m): inside nobody deals damage and the enemy that enters takes increasing damage from the garrison. On respawn, 5 s of protection that is lost on attacking. Finished with unit tests of the matrix and an integration one in which an enemy hit inside the camp does no damage. Includes the safe zones of the capitals and the matrix with guards; the protection on respawn belongs to CMB-15. Pure logic: the integration test is in CMB-10. Depends on: GUE-02, MUN-01. Closed (2026-11-06): `test_faction_rules` (the matrix), `test_combat_server::test_a_hit_inside_the_safe_area_of_the_victims_realm_does_no_damage` (the integration test), `test_combat_resolver` (safe areas protect their realm, an intruder can be hit from outside) and `test_npc_system::test_the_gate_guardians_kill_an_intruder_in_the_safe_area_in_under_ten_seconds`.
- [x] **GUE-05 Kill and assist credit** · F3 · important · M · server, shared — Damage log per victim on the server (`DamageLedger`, 900-tick window). On death, the killing blow goes to the last enemy attacker and the assist to whoever dealt at least 10 % of the damage; allies and self-harm do not count. An ENTITY_EVENT KILL (killer, victim, assistants, weapon) is emitted for the kill feed. Finished with unit tests of the distribution and of deaths by NPC or by falling. Depends on: GUE-03, CMB-10.
- [x] **GUE-07 Fortress definition and state machine** · F4 · essential · M · shared, content — `FortressDefinition` is a Resource in scripts/shared/war/, with the data in `data/fortresses/vado_gris.tres`: capture time, circle radius, gate health, garrison delay and guard respawn. `FortressState` is pure and runs in ticks: owner, phase (NEUTRAL, GARRISONED, VULNERABLE, CAPTURING, CONSOLIDATING), captain alive, gate health, active guards and the capture as a formula (start_tick, rate, progress_at_start). Finished with unit tests of all the transitions and that the formula gives the same progress as simulating tick by tick. Depends on: GUE-02.
- [x] **GUE-08 Fortress controller and capture circle on the server** · F4 · essential · M · server — `FortressController` (scripts/server/war/) creates a STRUCTURE NetEntity with ALWAYS_RELEVANT from the scene's markers and runs in the tick's NPC step. Every 6 ticks it counts the living players of each realm inside the circle's cylinder. With the captain dead, the capture takes 30 s with one ally and goes faster with more (cap ×2 with 4); it freezes if there is a living enemy inside. Includes debug commands (reset the fort, set owner, kill the captain) only with `--debug-commands`, and integration tests with fake players. Inactivity rule: whoever spends 60 s without sending inputs does not count in the circle. Decay of the empty circle as decided. Commands through TEC-19. Depends on: GUE-03, GUE-07, MUN-04, TEC-08, TEC-19. Done (F3): FortressController in scripts/server/war/ (STRUCTURE NetEntity with ALWAYS_RELEVANT per node of the fortresses group, presence every 6 ticks with the 60 s without inputs rule, advance every tick in step 4b, effects and OBJECT_STATE); commands fort_reset, fort_owner, fort_kill_captain and fort_capture_speed (ids 8 to 11, network with --debug-commands and console); tests with fake players in test_fortress_controller, test_fortress_server (capture neutral -> Vardheim -> Solmarch with the captain's double) and test_fortress_zone (the real Vado Gris).
- [x] **GUE-09 Networked OBJECT_STATE and fort view on the client** · F4 · essential · M · shared, server, client — Implements the reserved message 0x22 of networking.md: table of the fort's field_id, full state in the SPAWN and then only the changes, over reliable channel 3; bumps PROTOCOL_VERSION. On the client, `FortressView` applies the changes on their tick, computes the capture progress with the formula at `render_tick` and emits signals for the interface (owner, phase, progress, gate health). Finished with round-trip tests, malformed-message tests and a test_net_loopback that sees a full capture on the client. Generic OBJECT_STATE: gates, chests, resource nodes and weather reuse it. Depends on: GUE-08, TEC-04, TEC-10, TEC-13. Closed (2026-11-06): round trips in `test_net_messages_v3` (`test_object_state_layout_and_roundtrip`, `test_object_state_from_fortress_roundtrip`) and malformed ones (`test_object_state_rejects_malformed_fields`), `test_fortress_view` and `test_fortress_loopback::test_a_bot_client_sees_a_full_capture_as_object_states_and_war_events` (which also checks the client's FortressView owner).
- [ ] **GUE-10 Destructible fort gate (in progress)** · F4 · essential · M · shared, server, client — `FortGate` gains health: 6000 to start with, to be tuned so that 3 attackers break it in about 60 s. It is registered as a static hit target, without rewind. Only the owner's enemies damage it (or everyone if the fort is neutral). At 0 it falls (broken leaves, collision removed) and is repaired on changing owner or after 5 min without damage. Finished when a hit lowers the replicated health, the broken gate lets through the same on client and server and a test checks that the owner does not damage it. Includes the multipliers by damage type against the gate and leaves it closed by default (corrects docs/zones/vado_gris.md). Depends on: GUE-08, GUE-09, CMB-05, CMB-09, CMB-10. Done on the server (F3): the gate is a StructureTarget (oriented box, without rewind or criticals, multipliers by type in CombatRules, only the owner's enemies, HIT with the fortress's net_id); three attackers with the combo break it in 54.8 s; collision layers from s + 1 and deferred repair while someone overlaps the doorway. The client part is missing (FortressView and the exact prediction). Phase 4 review (war): only the repair of a **broken** gate is deferred; that of a whole (damaged) one is done at once even if someone is in the doorway (it does not change the collision) and a hit that removes health cancels what is pending. Before, the deferred repair was applied when the doorway became free even if the gate had taken hits afterwards: a defender standing still in the doorway of their own gate gave it back whole right after an assault, or closed a freshly broken gate. Tests in `test_fortress_controller` (three).
- [x] **GUE-11 Fort defenders: the Hollow and the realm garrison** · F4 · essential · S · server, content — Garrison of the owning realm: 4 guards and a captain with a tabard that appear 2 min after the capture, with the AI of MUN-09. The Hollow are MUN-10, the Ford Captain is MUN-11 and the leash is MUN-07's. Finished when, after capturing, the owner's garrison appears and defends the fort. Depends on: GUE-08, MUN-09, MUN-11, ART-03, CMB-10. Done (F4): garrison of the owning realm (4 `realm_guard` and 1 `realm_captain` with a tabard) that comes out of the Hollow's posts with the definition named by its `garrison_id` (same route and same alert group), appears at the end of the 2 min consolidation, replaces the defenders on changing owner and respawns with the fortress's timers (guards and captain). `test_npc_system` checks it after capturing. Provisional adjustment (F6b): the realm captain goes from 15,000 to 7500 health so that 20 bots recapture within Vado Gris's 30 min cycle (docs/fortress.md 8.3); it is reviewed with GUE-44 and with people. Reviewed with GUE-44: with 15,000 the bots also recapture within the cycle (five seeds, 2.6-17.4 min), so the adjustment is no longer needed for them; it is left at 7500 until tested with people.

- [x] **GUE-13 War alerts in the zone** · F4 · essential · S · server, shared — `WarEvent` catalog with texts in Spanish: fort attacked, captain fallen, capture started, capture contested, gate broken and fort captured. - [x] **GUE-14 Respawn at an owned fortress and in waves** · F4 · essential · M · server, client — On death, the server offers the realm's camp (always) and the own fortresses that are not in combat (no enemy damage to the gate or the defenders in the last 30 s). Respawn comes out in waves every 10 s and the server validates the choice. The client (RespawnPointList and the map) uses the same list (UI-21). Phase 4 review (war): automatic respawn (for whoever does not request it) also comes out in a wave, the first ≥ death + 30 s (`Combatant.auto_respawn_tick`); before it came out at exactly 30 s, outside a wave. The server's fake players (`--fake-players`, `SiegeRunner`, `load_test`, filler) request it on dying and `--bot` as soon as it can, like `SwarmBot`: all the earlier measurements of 20 per side were with individual respawns at 30 s; repeated in docs/fortress.md 8.3. Tests: `test_fortress_server` (two), `test_combatant` (two) and `test_online_main` (the `--bot`). Closed (2026-11-06): `test_fortress_server` (own fort only, neutral or attacked fort refused, waves every 10 s, the player appears at the interior markers) and `test_respawn_at_captured_fort` (after a real capture, the index the client computes from the map puts the player inside the fort; an enemy fort, an attacked one and a neutral one are refused by the server).
- [ ] **GUE-15 Siege bots to test the capture (in progress)** · F4 · essential · M · shared, tools — Per-realm objective modes in `BotBrain` for `--bots` and `--fake-players`: go to the fort by the bridge or by the ford, hit the gate, enter the circle, defend the walkway and attack the nearest enemy. Finished when `--bots=20` per side captures and recaptures the fort without intervention and a person can play against them. Depends on: GUE-08, TEC-15, CMB-06. Done (F6a): `BotBrain.Pattern.SIEGE` (at the end of the enum; bridge or ford route with its RNG, sprint, assault on the gate from 2.3 m, entry along the gate's axis, circle, fighting the enemy within 12 m and unsticking with a jump and detour; deterministic) for the server's fakes (`--fake-brain=siege`, which fill a shared `SiegeView` with `update_from_state`), for `--bots` (`SwarmBot`, which fills its own by decoding the ENTITY_SPAWN and OBJECT_STATE by hand) and for `--bot` (`FortressView`), all with `--bot-brain=siege`. `test_bot_brain_siege` (no physics), `test_siege_zone` (real Vado Gris: arrival by both routes in 24.6 and 37.5 s, gate, capture of the neutral with the captain's double, recapture) and `test_siege_clients` (ENet). The tool `tools/war/siege_battle.gd` does the long battle: 20 per side capture at 56 s and the other 20 recapture at 6 min, with `fort_kill_captain` as the only support. Done (F6b): without any intervention and with the real NPCs (`siege_battle.gd --npcs`). Siege bots first look for the NPCs guarding the fort while the captain lives, leave the zone of the warned hits (TELEGRAPH, also the Captain's unblockable) with a dodge and without blocking, drop chases that do not bring them closer and go around the palisade if they stay outside to one side (the enclosure, 40 x 40 m, is in `data/routes/vado_gris.tres`); `SwarmBot` and `--bot` also receive the alerts and the NPCs (since the F6b review, like the server's fakes, only the alerts of the NPCs that can damage them: before they also dodged those of their own realm's garrison). Fixed along the way: whoever was inside their closed gate hit outward without being hit (`hit_from_inside`). Measured with 20 per side and three seeds: **capture of the neutral always**, gate at 40-42 s, Ford Captain (30,000) at 71-73 s and capture at 86-88 s, without any Vardheim death (without the dodge, 14 unblockable hits and 7 deaths). **Recapture by Solmarch against the 20 of Vardheim and their garrison**: with the realm captain at 15,000, only one of three within the 30 min cycle (6.7 min); with the provisional adjustment to 7500 (`data/npcs/realm_captain.tres`), all three (6.7, 24.5 and 27.6 min). Short version in the suite: `test_siege_npcs` (12 bots, weaker NPCs only on that server, capture x30, without `fort_kill_captain`). Detail in docs/fortress.md 8.3 (F6b). Missing: for the recapture not to depend on the seed, which is lengthened by the circle's defenders against reinforcements arriving piecemeal (GUE-44, not the NPCs); defending the walkway (GUE-45); and the person who plays against them. It stays "in progress" because of those three things, not just the person. With GUE-44 (F6b: gathering at the camp and formation) the recapture with five seeds takes 2.4 to 12.8 min (median 2.6; before 4.4 to 27.6): it already always fits in the cycle, but still depends on the seed. With GUE-45 (F6b, package D) the bots defend the walkway: a quarter of the owner's guard it and 12.5 % of the attackers walk it; the recapture, with the same five seeds, 2.4 to 4.4 min (median 2.7). The person playing against them remains. Done (F6b, package E): the five battles repeated with `--battle-log` and the summary of each log give exactly the same (it is deterministic), and the load with NPCs (GUE-16) has the 10 real `SwarmBot` fight the NPCs and reach the fort before the window. F6b is done; GUE-15 stays "in progress" only because a person playing against them is missing. The measurements of 20 per side are with the server's fake players (`siege_battle.gd` with `SiegeRunner`, `--fake-brain=siege`: the same `BotBrain` as `--bots` and `--bot`), and that is how the `--bots=20` criterion has been read since F6a (docs/fortress.md 7); the whole battle only with `SwarmBot` over ENet remains in GUE-46, which does not block this item. F6b review (docs/fortress.md 8.3, "F6b review"): an attacker inside the fort no longer assaults the closed gate (repaired with them inside, or after their realm loses the fort with them on the walkway) and the chase without progress is measured per chase and per target; with the final code, the five seeds recapture at 202.4 / 399.8 / 151.4 / 152.2 / 144.3 s (median 2.5 min) and capture the neutral at 75-83 s. Integration of the abilities on F6b and its review (docs/fortress.md 8.3): with the phase 4 kit in the bots (the default template) and three seeds, capture of the neutral at 80.0-82.4 s and recapture at 156.1 / 146.9 / 295.6 s; the siege bots' bow only shoots with a firing line and on the walkway they look at the enemy and move along the axis.
- [x] **GUE-16 Fort combat load test** · F4 · important · S · tools, operations — `tools/load_test.gd` scenario with 32 vs 32 and 64 vs 64 bots fighting for 120 s at the gate and the circle. It measures the tick's p99, the µs per hit check with rewind, the KB/s per client and the cost of the fortresses. Finished when the result and the first per-battle player limit are recorded in docs (target: p99 < 8 ms with 64). Only adds the gate and circle scenario to TEC-15's load_test. Depends on: GUE-15, TEC-15, CMB-09. Done (F6a): `load_test.gd --siege` (54+10 and 118+10 players, 120 s with the bots already at the gate; `--run-at` opens the circle and the battle log feeds the summary) and p95 in the server summary. Measured without NPCs on a 4-core machine with another agent running: 32 vs 32, tick 10.6 / 15.3 / 19.1 / 27.8 ms (p50 / p95 / p99 / max), 36.1 KB/s per client, 0 substituted; 64 vs 64, 14.8 / 22.0 / 26.4 / 42.8 ms, 50.8 % of inputs substituted and 25 rewinds rejected. **The target of p99 < 8 ms with 64 is not met** (`simulate()` dominates, 77 µs per character; the `war` section costs 0.1 ms) and the first per-battle player limit at the fort is 64 (32 vs 32); recorded in docs/networking.md 14 and docs/fortress.md 8.3. Missing: repeating the measurement with F4's NPCs (F6b) and, with the levers of networking.md 14, looking at the p99 again. Since GUE-44 (F6b) the siege bots wait for their own at their camp and go in formation: the `SwarmBot` that enter after the fakes leave may wait up to 30 s, so when repeating it one must check that they arrive before the measurement window. Done (F6b): `load_test.gd --npcs` (with `--scenario=siege` or `--siege`) starts the server with the NPCs and the fort without aids (without the option, F6a's siege, now with an explicit `--no-npcs`); the summary gives the `npc_brains`, `npc` and `war` sections with their session mean, the NPCs of each metrics mark (the server writes them at the end of the line) and how many real bots reached the fort before the window (`fort_reached_tick` in the `SwarmBot` report): all 10, at 25-26.5 s. Measured with 32 vs 32, two passes with NPCs and two without them, alternated, on the 4-core machine without another suite: with NPCs, tick 10.6 / 14.8 / 17.4 / 27.5 and 9.4 / 13.5 / 15.2 / 23.0 ms (p50 / p95 / p99 / max), 36.2-36.3 KB/s per client, 0.00-0.03 % substituted and no rewind rejected; without NPCs, 9.1-9.9 / 12.8-14.2 / 15.0-16.9 / 24.9-27.9 ms. The 9 war NPCs (the 5 of the fort and the 4 Camp Guardians) cost ~1 ms of the tick while alive (brains with a p99 of 0.8-1.1 ms while the Hollow live, spawners 0.06 ms, their `simulate()` ~0.55 ms), of the order of the difference between two equal passes; `war`, 0.08 ms median. With 64 vs 64 and NPCs 16.2 / 22.8 / 27.0 / 39.6 ms and 38.6 % of inputs substituted was published, but those percentiles are of 118 players without any client: the swarm closed on its clock and the 128-player server, slower than real time, continued without the 10 real bots for the last stretch (with the 128, the marks gave p50 19.6 and p99 33.5 ms); the same happened to F6a's figure. F6b review: the swarm closes on the server's tick (`--quit-at-server-tick`) and the summary warns if the percentile stretch or the window marks lack the player peak; repeated with another suite running, 17.2 / 24.2 / 30.0 / 72.3 ms with the 128 in 90 % of the stretch, 32.1 KB/s per client, 0.00 % substituted and 44 rewinds rejected (the server ran at 87 % of real time; in the first, at 81 %). **The first per-battle player limit at the fort stays at 64 (32 vs 32), with the NPCs**; the target of p99 < 8 ms is not met and is left for the `simulate()` lever (TEC-35). Recorded in docs/networking.md 14 and docs/fortress.md 8.3 (F6b, package E). Integration of the abilities on F6b: those measurements are without the phase 4 kit; with it, Vado Gris's capacity is 40 and at the fort it was an estimate (the proposed measurement, `--siege --npcs`, carried no archers); `load_test.gd` requests its capacity with `--max-players`. Integration review: measured, `--scenario=siege --fakes=30 --bots=10 --seconds=120 --npcs`, 20 vs 20 with the NPCs: with the default template, tick 8.2 / 12.8 / 16.2 / 32.6 ms (p50 / p95 / p99 / max) and 34.4 KB/s per client; with half marksmen (`--fake-loadout=marksman:50`), 9.6 / 14.3 / 16.9 / 38.7 ms, 1.01 ms p99 of `combat_projectiles` and 36.1 KB/s; no input substituted in the window or rewind rejected. At the edge of the phase 4 criterion (16.7 and 1 ms): **the limit at the fort with the kit stays at 40 (20 vs 20), measured and without margin**; the lever, `simulate()` (TEC-35). The whole battle with archers remains in GUE-47 (docs/fortress.md 8.3 and docs/networking.md 14).
- [x] **GUE-17 Battle telemetry and fort tuning** · F4 · important · S · server, tools — The server writes the war events and deaths (tick, event, realm, position) to a JSON Lines file. `tools/war/summarize_battle.gd` extracts from it the time to capture, deaths per route (bridge, ford, walkway) and the time to break the gate. Done (F3): --battle-log (JSON Lines with war alerts, first hit on the gate and deaths) and tools/war/summarize_battle.gd, which counts one cycle for each reset to neutral. The documented test session with the numbers adjusted in vado_gris.tres is missing (it needs the siege bots of GUE-15). Done (F6a): bot sessions with `--battle-log` and their summary (`siege_battle.gd`: 20 per side; `load_test.gd --siege`): gate broken 14.9 s after the first hit with 20 attackers, neutral fort captured at 56 s (15 s capturing) and recaptured at 360 s by another 20, with 140 player deaths (131 on the esplanade and the courtyard, 9 at the ford); `BattleSummary` gains the `gate` death class. Done (F6b, package E): the session with the NPCs, five 20 vs 20 battles (`siege_battle.gd --npcs --battle-log`, seeds 1000 to 5000) with the summary of each log (`summarize_battle.gd`): capture of the neutral at 80.2-87.2 s (gate broken 10.7-12.0 s after the first hit, Ford Captain 28-36 s later), recapture at 146-265 s (the gate falls in 11.6-22.1 s in the middle of consolidation, 81.5 s once; the realm captain, 10-115 s after the garrison comes out), 360 player deaths (327 on the esplanade and the courtyard, 17 at the ford, 16 on the walkway, none on the bridge) and 48 NPC ones. With those numbers **the data in `vado_gris.tres` do not change**: the 9000 gate still gives GUE-10's figure and is not what takes longest in any phase (to weigh on the recapture it would need about eight times more health and 3 attackers would take more than 7 min), the 120 s consolidation is the floor of the recapture and does what its decision asks, the captain does not get to respawn (from its fall to the capture, 16-114 s) and the capture lasts 15-18.5 s. They remain provisional until tested with people (MUN-10, MUN-11). Detail in docs/fortress.md 8.3 (F6b, package E).

- [x] **GUE-42 Side balance in Vado Gris** · F4 · important · S · server, client — RealmSelect shows how many players each realm has. With --max-realm-diff=N, the server rejects with a translated text entry into a realm that is already N ahead, and --fake-players fills the short side with GUE-15 bots and removes them when people enter. Finished with an integration test of the rejection and the filling. Depends on: TEC-45, GUE-15. Done (F6a): `--max-realm-diff=N` (0 = no limit) with `RealmBalance` (only people count, against the realms the zone admits: entering *r* is rejected if `people[r] + 1 − min(others) > N`) and REJECT 9 after the identity provider; `realm_counts` in the WELCOME with the people of each realm not counting whoever enters; `FillerKeeper`: with N > 0 the automatic-realm fakes fill the side with the lower total, the oldest of the realm is removed for each person who enters the longer side and one enters when a person leaves (exact rule in docs/fortress.md 7). Tests: `test_realm_balance`, `test_server_auth`, `test_zone_server_net` (rejection with its text, count, fakes do not count) and `test_server_main` (real process with bots: two rejections, one fake removed and another replaced).
- [x] **GUE-43 Filler fakes do not take slots from real people** · F4 · important · S · server — With `--max-realm-diff` the filler fakes count toward the quota (`FULL` and `--max-players`): a server full of fakes rejects with "full" the person who should replace one, instead of removing a fake of their realm. Finished when a HELLO with the server full only of filler gets in and a fake of their realm is removed, with a test. Discovered in F6a. Depends on: GUE-42. Done (F6b): FULL only counts people and fakes that are not filler (`ServerAuth.validate(..., filler_seats)`). An accepted HELLO with the server full only because of filler gets in, and a fake yields its place to them. The oldest of the realm with the highest total yields, counting whoever enters: with the sides equalized, one of their realm. If their realm has no filler, the side with the highest total yields (decision in docs/fortress.md 7 and 8.3). It counts as the fake that removes its entry (one per person). F6b review: the fake no longer leaves when the HELLO arrives but when the person enters the world (`FillerKeeper.reserve_seat`, `release_seat`, `reserved_count`; FULL counts reserved seats as occupied). Before, a HELLO and a disconnect in a loop removed and recreated fakes without limit (DESPAWN and SPAWN to everyone; sometimes, of the opposing side), and replenishment without counting whoever was loading left the sides at 3 vs 1; now whoever is leaving while loading moves no fake. People never exceed `--max-players`. `ZoneServer.set_filler_keeper()` now carries the keeper's person registrations and removals, and `get_player_count()` no longer counts fakes with the removal requested. Tests: `test_realm_balance` (eight, and since the review the HELLO and disconnect loop, the two HELLOs with one leaving and the cap on reserved seats), `test_server_auth`, `test_zone_server_net` (with ENet: the server full of filler admits four and rejects the fifth, a fake that is not filler takes a seat, whoever leaves while loading moves no fake, five rounds of HELLO and disconnect without registrations or removals for whoever was already there, and the two HELLOs with one leaving end up one per realm) and `test_server_main` (GUE-42's process with `--max-players=6` and six fakes; before, "full" for the three people). Integration review of the abilities (docs/fortress.md 7 and 8.3): the oldest filler fake that is calm is removed (neither knocked down nor reviving or finishing; if there is none, the oldest) and its removal is never a death (`ZoneServer.retire_filler`: only DESPAWN, no DEATH or KILL); before, removing a knocked-down one added a death and a KILL because a person entered.
- [x] **GUE-44 Siege bot reinforcements and recapture balance** · F4 · important · S · shared, tools — Each `SIEGE` bot leaves on its own when respawning, so the attacker's reinforcements arrive in a trickle (30 s respawn in 10 s waves plus 25-37 s of marching) and the circle's defenders defeat them piecemeal: in the 20 vs 20 of `siege_battle.gd` there are 108 Solmarch deaths for 32 Vardheim ones and the recapture takes 6 min (8.8 min with 10 vs 10). Decide whether the attacker waits for their companions on the esplanade (rally point and leaving as a group) or whether it is a desired effect, and repeat the sessions with the NPCs. Finished when `--bots=20` per side recapture in a time recorded in docs/fortress.md 8.3 and the decision is made. Discovered in F6a. Depends on: GUE-15, GUE-17. Measured with the NPCs (F6b, docs/fortress.md 8.3): with 20 per side, the realm captain falls early and what lengthens the recapture is being left alone in the circle; depending on the seed it takes 6.7 to 27.6 min (with the realm captain at 7500) and the defenders win 3 to 1 in deaths. Done (F6b): **decided that it is not a desired effect**. Finding: characters do not collide with each other and bots following the same points end up stacked in the same place, where a sweeping hit hits everyone (with the gathering and no gaps, the first group of 20 died in 1.2 s without killing anyone: each defender hit hit 19); that, more than the circle, gave the defenders their 3 to 1. `BotBrain` (SIEGE): an attacker who starts or respawns at their camp waits at the rally point (the camp exit, the first point of their routes; task `RALLY`) until there are 8 of their realm, or 3 of their own fighting within 25 m of the circle (they go to help: between waves the captain heals), or at most the next departure of a common 30 s clock (5 s after a respawn wave); the group goes along the same route (that of that lap of the clock) and, in a group, each bot carries its own gap (from its seed, R2 sequence) in the march, at the gate, in the circle and at the rally. Deterministic and without a generator; the owner does not wait; without an ally counter (`set_ally_counter`) or with `set_rally(1)` everything stays as before. Counters: `ZoneServer._count_allies` (fakes), `SwarmBot` (`--bots`, by registrations and snapshots) and `BotAllySight` (`--bot`). `SiegeRunner.set_rally`/`rally_departures` and `siege_battle.gd --rally=N` (0 = as before) and `--rally-period`. Measured with 20 per side, real NPCs and five seeds (docs/fortress.md 8.3, F6b, package C): **recapture in 2.4 / 2.4 / 4.8 / 2.6 / 12.8 min** (median 2.6 min; before 6.7 / 27.6 / 24.5 / 4.4 / 5.6, median 6.7) and 1.3 attacker deaths for each defender one (before 2.7); capture of the neutral at 80-89 s. Waiting longer makes it worse (groups of 12: median 10.6 min; of 20: no recapture in 28 min) and gaps without rally give 7-12.5 min. With the realm captain at 15,000 they also recapture all five times within the cycle (2.6-17.4 min); its adjustment to 7500 is left until tested with people. It still depends on the seed: the battle is chaotic and is measured with five seeds. The measurements of 20 per side are with the server's fake players (`siege_battle.gd`); with `SwarmBot` over ENet there is no long battle (GUE-46). Tests: `test_bot_brain_rally` (fifteen), `test_siege_zone` (rally with the real server) and `test_siege_clients` (the `SwarmBot` count each other); the `--bot` counter (`BotAllySight`) had no test of its own until the F6b review, which adds `test_bot_ally_sight` and checks in `test_siege_clients` that `--bot` carries its counter. Integration review of the abilities: `BotAllySight` and `SwarmBot` also do not count the knocked-down (their published health is not 0; they look at the DOWNED action), like `ZoneServer._count_allies`.
- [x] **GUE-45 Owner bots defend the walkway** · F4 · nice-to-have · S · shared — The owner's `SIEGE` bots stay in the circle (the walkway is at 3.35 m and is climbed by ramps); with the NPCs' navigation mesh, a part of the owner's climbs to the walkway and from there strikes those at the gate. Finished when `siege_battle.gd`'s telemetry records deaths on the walkway and the zone test checks that the defenders climb. Discovered in F6a. Depends on: GUE-15, MUN-06. Done (F6b, package D; docs/fortress.md 8.3): **from the walkway one cannot reach those at the gate with melee weapons** (the weapon's sector reaches 0.2 to 2 m above the feet and the walkway is at 3.35 m, about 3.5 m from whoever hits the gate and behind the parapet), so **they guard it and fight whoever climbs**. A quarter of the bots (by their seed, `BotBrain.is_walkway_seed`) guard it when their realm is the owner: it enters, climbs the ramp on its side and waits at its post above the gate (±4 m according to its gap), ignoring those below; half of them (12.5 %) walk it once per life when attacking, on entering the fort, and then jump to the courtyard and go to the circle. The climb is a route precomputed in `data/routes/vado_gris.tres` (`walkway_height`, `walkway_path`, `walkway_ramps`), because the mesh only exists on the server and the brain is also used by `--bots` and `--bot`; `test_navmesh` checks that the NPCs' mesh (MUN-06) validates it segment by segment and `verify_vado_gris_routes.gd` walks it with the character (93.0 m, 18.9 s). Inside the enclosure, whoever is more than 1.6 m higher or lower is neither a target nor a warned hit to get out of; enemy finders weigh height by 4 (`BotBrain.target_distance_squared`); on the walkway, along the axis and turning corners, and without sideways dodges. Finding: the first climb crossed the edge of the circle and guards returning up disputed the capture (recapture at a median of 7.1 min); now it goes around the outside. Telemetry: `BattleSummary` counts the walkway's whole axis and NPC deaths per place, `siege_battle.gd --walkway=F` and its places report. Measured with 20 per side, NPCs and five seeds: **deaths on the walkway in all five battles** (2, 6, 3, 4 and 1; 11 Solmarch and 5 Vardheim) and recapture in 4.4 / 2.4 / 4.4 / 2.7 / 2.7 min (median 2.7; without walkway, 2.6 with tails of 8.2 and 20.7). Having the same 25 % of the attackers walk it doubled it (5.3 min); only with the circle active it left hardly any deaths on it. Tests: `test_bot_brain_walkway` (fourteen), `test_approach_routes`, `test_navmesh`, `test_battle_summary` and `test_siege_zone` (two of four of the owner's climb to their post at 13.55 m; an attacker climbs and fights a guard, and the death comes out on the walkway). The part that guards the walkway is a quarter on average: 4 to 6 of every 20 consecutive seeds (in the battle of seed 2000, 6 Vardheim guards). F6b review: a guard whose realm loses the fort no longer stays on the walkway "assaulting" the closed gate from above (it hit the air): it goes down to the courtyard and to the circle. Missing, outside GUE-45: hitting from the walkway with the bow (CMB-23) and reviewing the role with people.

- [x] **GUE-19 War persistence and PvP statistics** · F5 · essential · M · server, operations — The owner and phase of each fortress are saved in the database, with the timers converted to UTC time on the server (never in scripts/shared), and the capture history. Per character: deaths, kills, assists, captures, damage to gates and time defending. Finished when restarting the Vado Gris process keeps the owner and a half-done timer, and the statistics survive a reconnection. Depends on: GUE-05, GUE-12, TEC-22, TEC-25. Done (phase 5, P0): `fortresses`, `fortress_events` and `character_pvp_stats` (0002 and 0004). Done (phase 5, P3): the fortresses in the internal API (each zone reads and writes only its own, with `expected_version` and 409 `version_conflict`), the batch of war events without duplicates and the `pvp` section of the save, which lowers no total (platform.md §19.4); the zone (`FortressPersistence`, `ScoreKeeper`) is P6's. Done (phase 5, implementation review): a war event with an out-of-range date goes to `rejected` and a fortress record that `jsonb` does not admit is 400, instead of a 500 that the zone retried endlessly (platform.md §19.7). Done (phase 5, P4): the zone reads its instance's fortresses on startup (no response is not "no record") and has the PUT with one request in flight per fortress, `expected_version` and the resend after `version_conflict`, and the queue of war events (`ZonePlatform`; `FortressPersistence` applies and writes them, P6) (platform.md §19.8). Done (phase 5, P5): `CharacterSaver` with its sections, where P6 registers `ScoreKeeper`'s `pvp` one. In the "Exit", the final goes out before `ScoreKeeper.forget` (platform.md §19.9). Done (phase 5, P6): `FortressPersistence` in the zone: the record of §8 (schema 1, deadlines in the platform's Unix time, never in scripts/shared) is applied on startup before opening the port (CONSOLIDATING keeps owner and deadline, VULNERABLE the captain's and CAPTURING its capturer and its progress, which decays with the empty circle; empty list, initial state without a notice; another schema, with a notice), is written on each change of owner, phase or cycle and, if something else changed, every 60 s, only with the live instance, and notices 3, 6 and 7 go to the history; per character, `ScoreKeeper` adds `captures` (the capture list, without `gm_forced` or captures forced by a command) and `defend_ticks` (the owner with the circle active) and registers the `pvp` section (what was loaded plus the session, with the final before `forget`); the forced-change mark and `NetEntity.gm_forced`, which respawn removes (platform.md §19.11). The criterion is met: restarting the Vado Gris process keeps the owner and the half-done captain deadline (two processes against `FakeHttpServer`, also with the platform silent on startup, without opening the port or overwriting anything) and the `pvp` totals come back on reconnection (`FakeZonePlatform`; the platform never lowers them, P3). Not checked against the real Go store (P10).

The treasury is stored in the database, spent with `spend(realm, amount, reason)`, which leaves a record, and the balance is replicated to the interface. Finished with a test that the treasury matches the history of owners of a bot match. Depends on: GUE-20, GUE-19.
- [ ] **GUE-23 Castle: two gates, lord and 60 s capture** · F7 · essential · M · server, shared, content — Castle logic on FortressState: outer and inner gate in series (the inner one takes no damage until the outer one falls). Normal weapons do 10 % of their damage to gates and siege 100 %. The castle lord is in the keep and the circle lasts 60 s. Finished with Velmont in blockout, capturable from start to finish with debug commands and bots. Depends on: GUE-10, GUE-20, MUN-43.
- [ ] **GUE-25 Siege weapon framework and battering ram** · F7 · essential · L · shared, server, client, content — New SIEGE entity type: per-stream record and health through OBJECT_STATE. It is bought with Embers at a fortress or own camp and only deployed near an attackable fortress and outside safe zones. A player operates it, with inputs through INPUT_BATCH; it has health and disappears after 10 min without use. The ram is pushed by 1 to 4 players (faster with more), only damages gates, hits every 3 s and is vulnerable from the walkway. Finished when a group of bots breaks Velmont's outer gate with it. Depends on: GUE-10, GUE-22, GUE-23.
- [ ] **GUE-26 Catapult and ballista** · F7 · important · L · shared, server, client — The catapult throws a deterministic parabolic projectile with a trajectory previewed for the operator: range of 40-120 m, 8 s reload, full damage to walls and gates and reduced area damage to players. The ballista fires a direct bolt with a 4 s reload and heavy damage to siege weapons and players. Finished with tests that the trajectory is the same on client and server and that damage depends on the target type. Depends on: GUE-25.
- [ ] **GUE-27 Destructible walls and breaches** · F7 · important · M · server, client, content — Each castle has weak wall sections (`breakable_walls` group) whose health only drops with siege. When they fall they turn into a ruined mesh with a passable breach, with the same collision on client and server, and are rebuilt on changing owner. Finished when a catapult opens in Velmont a breach through which the bots enter. Depends on: GUE-23, GUE-26.
- [ ] **GUE-28 Each fort's own effects** · F7 · important · M · server, client — Each fort gives its advantage to the owning realm. The Brenn Watchtower reveals on its map groups of 3 or more enemies within 150 m. Odd's Quarry makes its siege weapons 30 % cheaper. Ilse Shrine is an advanced respawn point with a 5 s wave. Kerth's Mine adds extra Embers. Finished with one test per effect that checks it activates and turns off on changing owner. Depends on: GUE-14, GUE-21, GUE-22, UI-33.
- [ ] **GUE-29 War state in all zones: map and alerts** · F7 · important · M · server, operations — The Eldrun process publishes a summary on the cluster bus every time something changes: owners, attacked fortresses, Ember chains, treasury and active blessings. Each zone process forwards it to its clients, so the war map and the alerts ("Velmont está siendo atacado" (Velmont is being attacked)) reach the capital and the PvE zones of the affected realm. Finished with a multi-process test in which a capture in Eldrun appears on the map of a client who is in the capital. Depends on: GUE-20, TEC-29.
- [ ] **GUE-30 Realm blessings** · F7 · important · M · server, shared — Catalog of blessings paid with Embers (experience +10 %, loot +10 %, 10 % discount at merchants, etc.), each with cost, duration and activation rule (see open questions). They apply to all the realm's characters in any zone and are seen in the interface. Finished when a blessing activated in Eldrun applies in the realm's capital in under 5 s. Depends on: GUE-22, GUE-29, CMB-20, PRG-13, PRG-21, PRG-23.
- [ ] **GUE-31 Aldcor and the rekindled Flame** · F7 · important · L · server, shared, content — Only a realm with 2 of the 3 castles can attack Aldcor. It has three rings of broken wall with breaches, defenders and a capture in the Flame chamber according to the rule that is decided. On capturing it, the Flame burns with the realm's color for a few hours and gives a greater blessing, and the other two realms attack with a bonus until it goes out. Finished with a full cycle with bots: blocked access, capture, blessing, rivals' bonus and extinguishing. Depends on: GUE-21, GUE-23, GUE-30.
- [ ] **GUE-35 Anti-abuse: arranged captures and kill farming** · F7 · important · M · server, operations — The reward drops on recapturing the same fortress within 30 min and on capturing without opposition (no player defenders or damage received). After 3 deaths of the same victim at the hands of the same killer in 10 min, renown goes to 0. Whoever sends no inputs in 60 s stops counting in the circle. Suspicious patterns, such as two realms swapping a fort, are logged for review. Finished with tests of each rule. The inactivity rule belongs to GUE-08 and the anti-farming, the single one, to PRG-09. Depends on: PRG-09, GUE-17.
- [ ] **GUE-36 Population balance between realms** · F7 · important · M · server, client — Count of active players per realm in Eldrun and on the server. If a realm is below 75 % of the largest, it earns more renown and its fortresses have reinforced NPC guards, never more player damage. Character creation recommends the realm with the lowest population and gives it a welcome bonus. Finished with a test that the bonus activates and is withdrawn as the counts vary. Depends on: GUE-20, PRG-09, TEC-24.
- [ ] **GUE-37 War ranks and rankings** · F8 · important · M · server, client — Ten ranks by accumulated renown, with a title visible on the nameplate and small non-combat advantages (e.g. unlocking siege weapons). Weekly and overall rankings per realm and server: kills, captures and renown. Finished with the rankings generated from the database and visible in the game. The ranks belong to PRG-32; here, the rankings and their panel. Depends on: PRG-09, PRG-32, UI-09.
- [ ] **GUE-38 Anti-multi-account and espionage** · F8 · important · M · server, operations — Detects accounts that share an IP or machine fingerprint and play at the same time in enemy realms within Eldrun, and accounts that log in only to inflate the population or to see positions. Generates alerts for the GMs with the history; it blocks nothing automatically except the one-realm-per-account rule. Finished with a test report on simulated data. Depends on: GUE-35, TEC-23, TEC-24.
- [ ] **GUE-39 War seasons** · F9 · nice-to-have · M · server, operations — 3-month seasons with their own ranking, cosmetic rewards at the close and an archive of results; optionally, the fortresses return to neutral as decided. Finished with a simulated season close that distributes the rewards and empties the rankings. Depends on: GUE-37, GUE-19.

### Social and community (SOC)

- [x] **SOC-01 Character name rules** · F2 · important · S · shared, server — NameRules in scripts/shared with validate(name) and normalize_key(name) (lowercase and without accents for comparison): length, only Latin letters with accents and ñ, initial capital and a list of reserved names (GM, Admin, realms, NPCs and places from world.md); ServerAuth uses it for the REJECT NAME and rejects a name already connected in the zone, so that whispers by name are not ambiguous. Finished when test_name_rules covers valid and invalid names and collisions by accents or capitals. Depends on: TEC-08.
- [x] **SOC-02 Social design document (docs/social.md)** · F3 · important · S · content — docs/social.md sets what crosses between realms (names, chat, gestures, inspection), channels and ranges, group and band sizes, commands in Spanish, rate limits, log retention and the sanction scale. Finished when it resolves the open questions that block phases 3-4 and vision.md links to it. Depends on: SOC-01.
- [x] **SOC-03 Chat protocol** · F4 · important · M · shared — CHAT_SEND (C→S) and CHAT_MESSAGE (S→C) messages in scripts/shared/net over reliable channel 3 (types 0x40 onward), with channel (say, zone, whisper, group, band, guild, realm, system, GM), text of up to 255 characters and 768 B, sender and recipient by name; decode returns null if malformed and bumps PROTOCOL_VERSION. Finished with round-trip tests, corrupt-packet tests and that no message exceeds 1200 B. Depends on: SOC-02, TEC-04. Done: `NetMessages.ChatSend` (5 B + recipient + text, ≤ 821 B) and `NetMessages.ChatMessage` (11 B + sender + recipient + text, ≤ 875 B) with types 0x40 and 0x41 over reliable channel 3; `ChatChannel` (fixed ids of the nine channels) and `ChatText` (limits of 255 characters and 768 B and sanitizing) in `scripts/shared/social/`; `PROTOCOL_VERSION` goes to 5. `test_chat_messages` (13: byte layout, round trip, limits, maxima, corrupt, truncated at every length, fuzz) and `test_chat_text` (7) check it.
- [x] **SOC-04 Chat routing on the zone server** · F4 · important · M · server — ChatRouter in scripts/server with /decir (say) at 30 m, zone, in-process whisper and system messages, which never sends a client the text of another realm, sanitizes the text (control characters, BBCode, spaces) and limits per peer (1 message/s, burst of 5, repeats discarded) adding warnings to the existing RateLimiter. Finished with a loopback integration test with two clients of one realm and one of the other. Depends on: SOC-03, TEC-08. Done: `ChatRouter`, `ChatPeerState`, `SocialPeers` and `SocialService` in `scripts/server/social/` (`ZoneServer` only creates the service and passes it types 0x40 to 0x4F); say at 30 m, zone, whisper (to the enemy and to whoever does not exist: the same reply "no está conectado" (is not connected)), group (with its slot for SOC-06) and system; sanitizing with `ChatText`; rates from docs/social.md section 5 with the warning "Hablas demasiado rápido" (You speak too fast) and a peer warning for every five discards; `/r` is resolved by the client (decision in social.md 12.2). Client: `NetClient.send_chat()` and the `chat_received` signal. `test_chat_router` (24) and `test_chat_loopback` (8, with three ENet bots: the one of the other realm receives no CHAT_MESSAGE or a packet with the text, checked in the bytes).
- [x] **SOC-05 Chat window and commands** · F4 · important · M · client — Chat box in the HUD: Enter opens the line and suspends movement and camera, Esc closes it, filters per channel with its own color, history with the up arrow, fade at rest, commands in Spanish (/d, /z, /s name, /r, /ayuda) and the server's NOTICE in the system channel; the text of other players is shown without interpreting BBCode. Finished with tests of the command parser and a demo capture with messages. Depends on: SOC-04, UI-03, UI-04. Done: `scripts/client/chat/` with `ChatBox` (tabs per channel, line, fade at rest, Enter and `/` open, Esc closes, history with the up arrow), `ChatCommandParser` (`/d /z /g /s /r /ayuda` and the group ones, case- and accent-insensitive; unknown: "Comando desconocido. Escribe /ayuda." (Unknown command. Type /ayuda.)), `ChatLog`, `ChatStyle` and `ChatInputHistory`; mounted in the networked HUD. While typing, the cursor is released and game input is suspended (`LocalPlayerController.set_input_blocked`: the current windows only released the cursor, not the movement). NOTICEs come out in the system channel and the text of other players is painted with `add_text` (never BBCode). `test_chat_command_parser` (9), `test_chat_box` (21, with synthetic messages and a fake client) and the capture `docs/screenshots/chat_demo.png` (`scenes/tests/chat_preview.tscn`; with `xvfb-run` it can be taken without a window of its own, see social.md 12.3). Channel colors, sizes and texts provisional (pending from the user).
- [x] **SOC-06 Groups: server and protocol** · F4 · important · M · shared, server — PartyManager on the zone server and PARTY_* messages (invite, accept, decline, leave, kick, change leader, state): same realm only, maximum 5 (constant), invitation that expires at 60 s, leader inherited on leaving, /g channel and compact state of the members (health and position at 2 Hz even if they are outside the interest radius). Finished with integration tests of all the flows, including the invitation to an enemy being rejected. Depends on: SOC-04, TEC-08, CMB-10. Done: eight PARTY_* messages (0x48 to 0x4F: invite, respond, leave, kick, hand over command, the invitation, the member list and the compact state) and `NetProtocol.PARTY_MAX_SIZE` (5) and shared `PartyRules`; `PartyManager` in `scripts/server/social/` (same realm, 5 members, 60 s invitations with 5 pending per sender and their rate bucket, inherited leader, group of one dissolved, disconnection, `/g` channel through the `ChatRouter`) and the members' state (health, stamina, status bits and position) at 2 Hz to all members even if they are outside the interest radius. `NetClient` sends (`send_party_*`) and stores in `PartyState`. `test_party_messages` (12), `test_party_manager` (20, no network) and `test_party_loopback` (7, with ENet bots: all the flows, the invitation to an enemy rejected without them noticing and a member at 210 m with a 100 m interest policy).
- [ ] **SOC-07 Social menu on players (in progress)** · F4 · important · M · client — Since there is no target selection, social actions come from the player under the aim point (ray against the remote views, up to 20 m, with a key) or from a name clicked in the chat with the cursor free: menu with invite, whisper and, when they exist, inspect, add friend, ignore and report, without showing with an enemy the options that do not apply. Finished when inviting and whispering work from both origins. Depends on: SOC-05, SOC-06, TEC-09, UI-09. Done so far: the aim-point origin. `SocialTarget` (ray against the capsule of each remote player being drawn, at 20 m or less from the local player and not from the camera, the nearest along the ray; only living allies, with a person's name and without a wall in front, with the occlusion of the nameplates: NPCs, enemies, the dead and fake players are ignored) and `SocialMenu` (a `UIWindow`: invite to the group and whisper with an ally, only whisper with a group member or with the full group), with the `social_menu` key (G, provisional: it was T until phase 4 gave T to the stamina potion, docs/abilities.md §11.7) picked up by the `HUD`; with no ally like that under the crosshair, the menu does not open and the chat always gives the same notice, also with an enemy or a wall in front (adversarial review: before it gave away an enemy behind a wall). Inviting sends PARTY_INVITE and whispering opens the chat line with `/s name `. `test_social_menu` (11). Missing: the second origin, a name clicked in the chat with the cursor free (chat lines do not yet have clickable names), and the options of later phases (inspect, add friend, ignore and report).
- [x] **SOC-08 Group interface** · F4 · important · M · client — Group frames (name, health, stamina, leader's crown, dimmed if far or dead), invitation notice with accept and decline by keyboard, commands /invitar, /salir, /expulsar, /lider and nameplates with group color. Finished when a demo with four grouped bots shows the correct frames. Depends on: SOC-06, SOC-07, UI-07, UI-10. Done: `scripts/client/party/` with `PartyFrames` and `PartyFrame` (name with the realm's color, drawn leader's crown, health, stamina and distance; dimmed if more than 100 m away, dead or without data), `PartyInvitePrompt` (who invites, seconds remaining and the real keys for accept and decline: actions `party_accept` and `party_decline`, Y and N provisional) and `PartyHud`, which mounts them in the HUD and translates `/invitar`, `/salir`, `/expulsar` and `/lider` from the chat; nameplates paint group members with the group color (`Nameplates.set_party`). `test_party_hud` (8, with synthetic states), `test_party_demo` (the criterion's demo: a server and five ENet bots, the first's frames show the five, the crown, the health, the distance and the dimming when one moves away and when another leaves) and a new case in `test_nameplates`. Sizes, colors and keys provisional (pending from the user). It does not wait for the SOC-07 menu: the notice and the frames do not depend on it.
- [ ] **SOC-09 Group members on compass, minimap and map** · F4 · important · S · client — Icons of the group members on the compass, the minimap and the zone map, with an arrow at the edge if they are out of view, interpolating the 2 Hz positions of SOC-06. Finished when a member at 250 m, outside the interest radius, still appears in their place. Depends on: SOC-06, UI-19, UI-20.
- [ ] **SOC-10 Pings and tactical marks** · F4 · important · M · shared, server, client — Ping of the point under the aim point with a type (attack, defend, danger, gather), validated on the server (at most 300 m, 1 every 2 s with a burst of 3) and shown to the group for 6 s in the world, the compass and the minimap with sound, plus up to 4 persistent marks from the leader (e.g. over the fort gate). Finished with a rate-limit test and a demo in which another client sees the ping. Depends on: SOC-06, SOC-09, UI-19, ART-10, ART-12.
- [x] **SOC-13 Unique and reserved names in the database** · F5 · essential · M · server, shared — Unique index on NameRules's normalized key, availability check on character creation, 30-day retention of the name of deleted characters, GM-editable blacklist and a forced-rename mark applied on entering. Finished with collision tests by accents and capitals and of two simultaneous creations with the same name. Decided in platform.md §0 and §4.4: the deleted character can be restored for 7 days and its name stays reserved those 7 and 30 more (test ones, with under 24 h of life and 1 h played, only the 7); the blacklist is edited by `admin --actor <email> names block|unblock`. Depends on: SOC-01, TEC-22. Done (phase 5, P0): `character_names` (normalized key unique per world, with dated reservations), `name_blacklist` and `characters.must_rename` (0002_characters); the logic and the collision tests are P2's. Done (phase 5, P2): `NameRules` in Go (`internal/names`, with the `names` section of `progression_rules.json` and the same 119 cases as `test_name_rules.gd`), the key unique per world on create and rename, `name-check`, the name reservation on deletion (7 days and 30 more; test ones, only the 7), the blacklist (422 `reserved`) and `must_rename` (`enter` answers 409 `rename_required` and one renames with `POST /v1/characters/{id}/rename`), with collision tests by accents and capitals ("Anéira", "ANEIRA", "Aneira") and of simultaneous creations with the same name (platform.md §19.3). The command that edits the blacklist (`admin names block|unblock`) arrives with P7a. Done (phase 5, P7a): `admin --actor <email> names block|unblock <name>` (the NameRules key, letters only; it warns about characters that are already named that, which are renamed with `sanction add … rename`), audited (platform.md §19.5). Done (phase 5, P9a): the row of a reserved name is deleted when the reservation ends (platform.md §19.15).
- [x] **SOC-14 GM roles and audit log** · F5 · essential · M · server, operations — Role per account (player, moderator, GM, administrator) in the database; the TEC-19 commands move to the chat (/gm ...) with permission per role validated on the server, visible tag and invisible mode for GMs, and each command is audited (who, when, zone, target, arguments). Finished when a player without a role receives "comando desconocido" (unknown command) and the audit can be queried. Depends on: TEC-19, TEC-23. Done (phase 5, P0): `accounts.role` and `audit_log`, with the index that `set-role` searches (0001 and 0005); roles, commands and query, in P7a and P7b. Done (phase 5, P3): the zone's audit batch (`POST /internal/v1/audit`), validated entry by entry against the instance (no `cli` or `platform` rows, nor keys of another instance, nor `op:<op_id>` of other blocks), and the platform rows `zone_replace`, `sanction` and `sanction_denied` (platform.md §19.4). Done (phase 5, P7a): the `admin` command with mandatory `--actor` (admin, or moderator to read) and its row in `audit_log` for each command, also reads, without emails or names in `args`; `set-role` revokes the account's sessions (the heartbeat kicks their character and they re-enter with the new role) and leaves the fixed row that S2 reads (`sanctions.RoleAt`); the audit is queried with `admin audit --since … [--actor-name …] [--target …]` (platform.md §19.5). The zone half of the criterion, "comando desconocido" without a role, and `/gm` and invisible are missing (P7b). Done (phase 5, P4): the role reaches the zone with the claim and the ENTITY_SPAWN of a moderator or higher carries bit 3 of the header for the tag (protocol 7; platform.md §19.8); the tag on the client, in P8. Done (phase 5, implementation review): an audit entry with data the database rejects (NUL or broken UTF-8 in `args`, out-of-range date) goes to `rejected` instead of taking down the batch with a 500, and the save's `op:<op_id>` row is not overwritten (platform.md §19.7). Done (phase 5, P7b): the zone: each command declares its `min_role` in the `CommandRegistry`; `/gm …` (through the chat's GM channel) with the claim's role, and without it or with one that does not exist, "Comando desconocido. Escribe /ayuda."; DEBUG_COMMAND with a platform also requires the role (without it, a notice); the moderation commands (`aviso`, `silenciar`, `expulsar`, `suspender`, `renombrar`, `banear`, `invisible`, `visible`); the invisible GM (outside interest, without damage, without circle or scoreboard or rewards); `gm_forced` with `/gm teleport`, `/gm visible` and DEBUG_COMMAND 1, 2 and 4; and each executed command to the audit batch with `actor_account_id`, `source` `zone` or `console` and the target by id (platform.md §19.12). With P7a's part the criterion is met: a player without a role receives "comando desconocido" (`test_zone_moderation`) and the audit is queried with `admin audit`. The tag on the client is P8's. Done (phase 5, P9a): the audit is deleted after a year (job `privacy_retention`, platform.md §19.15).
- [x] **SOC-15 Persistent chat log** · F5 · important · S · server, operations — Each routed message (channel, sender's character_id, whispers' recipient, zone and copy, text and UTC time; the group of a group message is not stored, platform.md §9.2) is stored in an append-only log or in the database, with automatic deletion on reaching the agreed retention. Finished when a character's history between two dates is retrieved and what has expired disappears. Depends on: SOC-04, TEC-22. Done (phase 5, P0): the `chat_messages` table with `zone_channel` (0005_social). Done (phase 5, P3): the zone's chat batch (`POST /internal/v1/chat`) with `world_id`, `zone_id`, `zone_channel` and `sender_account_id` set by the platform (platform.md §19.4); the query and the retention are P7a's and P9a's. Done (phase 5, P7a): `admin --actor <email> chat --character <name> --from … --to …` (what they sent and the whispers they received, with zone and copy, audited) and the 30-day retention (job `chat_retention`, every hour, in batches of 10,000), with the criterion's test on the platform (history between two dates and what has expired disappearing, platform.md §19.5); it is ticked when the zone sends each message's batch (P7b). Done (phase 5, implementation review): a message with an out-of-range date goes to `rejected` and does not jam the batch (platform.md §19.7). Done (phase 5, P7b): each distributed message of a character goes to the zone's chat batch (`{channel, sender_character_id, target_character_id, text, sent_at_unix}`, a whisper's recipient by id), so the criterion is met end to end: the zone sends it (`test_zone_moderation`, `test_chat_moderation`) and the platform stores it, returns it between two dates and deletes it on expiry (P7a). With a platform, the chat state (`ChatPeerState`) is per character and is kept 5 min after leaving (platform.md §19.12).
- [x] **SOC-16 Persistent ignore list** · F5 · important · M · server, client — Up to 100 ignored players stored in the database; the server discards from the ignored player whispers, invitations (group and later guild and friendship) and mail, and also removes them from the recipients of public channels (the client does not know which characters share an account; platform.md §9.3), with /ignorar, /designorar and an option in the social menu. Finished with an integration test in which an ignored player cannot whisper, invite or reach their zone channel. Depends on: SOC-07, TEC-25. Done (phase 5, P0): the `ignores` table (0005_social). Done (phase 5, P3): `POST` and `DELETE /internal/v1/ignores` (up to 100, the name resolved in the instance's world) and the ignored accounts in the claim (platform.md §19.4); the filter in the zone is P7b's. Done (phase 5, P7b): `/ignorar` and `/designorar` in the zone (`POST`/`DELETE /internal/v1/ignores`, the account to the filter at once) and the claim's ignored accounts; the server discards from the ignored player the messages of all channels (the sender of a whisper receives their copy: they do not know they are being ignored) and their group invitations (they stay pending without reaching the invitee, who cannot accept them). The criterion's integration test is `test_zone_moderation` (an ignored player does not whisper, invite or reach their zone channel); the client sends the commands (platform.md §19.12). The social menu option is missing (SOC-07).
- [x] **SOC-17 Player reports** · F5 · important · M · server, client — /reportar and a social menu option with category (language, harassment, cheating, bot, offensive name, other) and comment; the server attaches the reported player's last messages that the reporter saw, zone, position and tick, and queues it in the database with a maximum of 1 report per target every 10 min and 10 per hour. Finished with a test that the report reaches the queue with its evidence. Depends on: SOC-07, SOC-15, TEC-22. Done (phase 5, P0): `reports` and `report_evidence` (0005_social). Done (phase 5, P3): `POST /internal/v1/reports` with the evidence copied to `report_evidence` and the limits of 1 per target every 10 min and 10 per hour (platform.md §19.4); `/reportar` and the message ring in the zone are P7b's. Done (phase 5, P7a): the queue is reviewed with `admin --actor <email> reports list|show|close` (reads, for moderator; closing, for admin; all audited), and a test sees the report in the queue with its evidence (platform.md §19.5). Done (phase 5, P7b): `/reportar name category [comment]` in the zone, with the reported player's last 20 messages that the reporter received in 10 min (a ring of 100 per receiver), the zone, the copy, the tick and the positions; the zone already throttles 1 per target every 10 min and 10 per hour, and the platform's 429 says how long to wait. With P7a's test (the report in the queue with its evidence) and the zone's (`test_zone_moderation`: the report goes out with its evidence), the criterion is met (platform.md §19.12). The social menu option is missing (SOC-07). Done (phase 5, P9a): report retention: closed ones, with their evidence, are deleted a year after closing, and an unclosed one closes itself after a year (`dismissed`, "caducado" (expired)) and counts from there (platform.md §19.15).
- [x] **SOC-18 Sanctions and community rules** · F5 · essential · M · server, content — Warning, mute, temporary suspension, permanent ban and forced rename, with reason, author, expiry and linked reports, applied on authenticating (rejection with reason and end date), in ChatRouter (remaining mute time) and in the name; rules and sanction scale in docs/normas.md and in a screen that is accepted when creating the account. Finished with tests of each sanction and its expiry. Depends on: SOC-14, SOC-17, TEC-23, TEC-43. Done (phase 5, P0): `sanctions` and `sanction_reports` (0005_social). Done (phase 5, P1): the rules (`platform/legal/rules-1.md`, with the scale) are accepted when creating the account; a valid suspension or ban leaves, after the password, only the privacy session, and the game routes answer 403 with the reason and the end date (platform.md §4.2, §19.2). Done (phase 5, P2): the forced rename (`characters.must_rename`): `enter` answers 409 `rename_required` and the player chooses another name with `POST /v1/characters/{id}/rename`, and the old one stays reserved 30 days (platform.md §19.3); the sanction that sets it is P7a's. Done (phase 5, P3): `POST /internal/v1/sanctions` with the platform's authorization (author's lease, minimum role per type and duration, target of lower role; otherwise, 403 and a `sanction_denied` row), the mute in the claim and in the heartbeat, and the suspension, ban and requested deletion as `revoked` of the heartbeat and 403 `account_blocked` in the claim (platform.md §19.4); the CLI and the expiry tests of each sanction are P7a's. Done (phase 5, P7a): the same authorization for the zone and the CLI (`sanctions.Create`), `admin sanction add|revoke|list` with each sanction's state, the warnings in the claim (`account.warnings`, for the NOTICE on entering, once) and tests of each sanction and its expiry with the injected clock: warning (comes out once), mute and suspension (until their end), ban (permanent until revoked) and rename (until renamed) (platform.md §19.5). The zone part of the criterion is missing: the mute in `ChatRouter`, the NOTICE and `/gm` (P7b). Done (phase 5, implementation review): the claim only says the account is sanctioned (and why) with a valid ticket for that character, and a warning is not considered seen until the character's first save in the world (platform.md §19.7). Done (phase 5, P7b): the zone part: the mute in `ChatRouter` ("Estás silenciado N min más." (You are muted for N more min.), from the claim, from the heartbeat's `mutes` and from a sanction of this zone at once), the NOTICE of warnings on entering, sanctions through `/gm` (`aviso`, `silenciar`, `suspender`, `renombrar`, `banear`; the platform authorizes them) with what applies to whoever is in the zone (the rename kicks them with their final save; suspension and ban request a heartbeat at once) and their replies (platform.md §19.12). With P7a's tests of each sanction and its expiry, the criterion is met. Done (phase 5, P9a): the sanctions of a purged account are deleted a year after the purge, and the email fingerprint of a sanctioned account that is deleted (up to 90 days after the sanction's end; a ban, a year) prevents registering it again until it expires, when the hourly job deletes it (platform.md §19.15).

### Technology and servers (TEC)

- [x] **TEC-01 Test runner and architecture tests** · F1 · essential · S · tools, shared — tests/run_tests.gd discovers test_*.gd, fails on any engine error and has a filter and time limits. test_architecture.gd prevents scripts/shared from using Input, cameras, UI, clock or global randomness. It already exists; TEC-10 extends it to scripts/server and scripts/client.

The FULL_GAME collision is the same on client and server. test_zones and test_terrain3d_headless already load Vado Gris without a window, which is the base of ServerMain.
- [x] **TEC-03 Continuous integration** · F2 · essential · M · operations, tools — GitHub Actions on Linux with headless Godot 4.7.2: checkout (LFS objects were cached until the move to normal blobs), --import and the full run_tests.gd (also network and processes) on every push and PR, and logs and --report reports as artifacts. Finished when a broken test blocks the merge and the run lasts under 15 min. Essential before phase 5. Written in `.github/workflows/tests.yml`, validated with actionlint but not run: the duration (under 15 min) remains to be checked on GitHub and "Suite completa" (full suite) to be marked as a required check in the branch protection. Depends on: TEC-01.
- [x] **TEC-04 Network A: shared base and protocol** · F2 · essential · L · shared — Package A of docs/networking.md:
- changes in PlayerState and PlayerCharacter: to_f32, equals_exact, yaw in f32 and optional events;
- new classes: NetProtocol (version 1, channels, Realm, sim_hash, neutral_input), NetMath, NetZones, EntityRecord, NetMessages and BotBrain.
Finished when test_net_messages passes (round trip, truncated, fuzz of 10,000 buffers, quantization of 5 mm or less) and test_architecture stays green. Depends on: TEC-01.
- [x] **TEC-05 Network B: transport and network simulator** · F2 · essential · M · shared — NetTransport over ENetMultiplayerPeer with its own SceneMultiplayer, used only to authenticate and for send_bytes. It has 3 channels and timeouts, sends nothing before peer_connected and carries app/wire counters. NetSim works in ticks and with a seed; losses and duplicates only affect the unreliable channels. Finished when test_net_sim passes: it is reproducible, the reliable ones keep FIFO order and the delay stays in range. Depends on: TEC-04.
- [x] **TEC-06 Network C1: pure server pieces** · F2 · essential · L · server — Pieces without network:
- InputQueue: window, ack, lead time and substitution without invented edges;
- StateHistory: ring of 64 ticks in packed arrays;
- SnapshotBuilder: 1200 B at most, priority accumulator and staggered sends;
- plus base InterestPolicy, tick-based RateLimiter and SpawnPicker.
Finished when test_input_queue, test_state_history, test_snapshot_builder (properties with 200 entities for 600 ticks) and test_rate_limiter pass. Depends on: TEC-04.
- [x] **TEC-07 Network D: client simulation** · F2 · essential · L · client — TickSync, InterpolationClock, ClientPrediction, CorrectionSmoother and RemoteInterpolator, with their unit tests. Finished when test_prediction, with two SubViewports of separate physics, gives an identical encode() for the same inputs, reconciles exactly after a substitution and smooths without jumps of more than 1 mm. Depends on: TEC-04.

The --local and --bot modes, ClientConfig, RealmSelect and NetDebugOverlay are TEC-45. The networkless phase 1 mode stays the same. - [x] **TEC-13 Combat protocol and versioning policy** · F3 · essential · M · shared — PROTOCOL_VERSION 2:
- reliable ENTITY_EVENT (0x23) for hit, block, damage, death and respawn;
- block with the own health and stamina (bit 3 of flags);
- action byte in EntityRecord and combat parameters inside sim_hash.
The rule is documented in networking.md: every format or simulation change bumps the version and REJECT VERSION asks "actualiza el cliente" (update the client). Finished when the extended test_net_messages and test_net_loopback pass. Depends on: TEC-10, CMB-03, CMB-04. Closed (2026-11-06): `test_net_messages` (ENTITY_EVENT hit, death, kill and respawn, the action byte of EntityRecord, the snapshot's own-state block with health and stamina, `sim_hash` depending on the combat parameters), `test_net_loopback::test_a_protocol_version_mismatch_is_rejected` and the rule in networking.md and combat.md section 6; the protocol has moved on to version 7 since.

- line of sight with a raycast against the current world.

Only combat anti-cheat validations (stamina, rate and reach), suspicion score and new cheating bot modes; the rewind and the 95 % test are CMB-09's. The validation of ability cooldown and its cheating bot mode arrive with CMB-19 (the only cooldown of phase 3, the parry's, is imposed by the shared simulation and tested by test_action_machine). Depends on: CMB-09, TEC-13, TEC-46. Done: modes `--bot-cheat=rewind` (40 ticks of view), `reach` (attacks from 5 m) and `rate` (mashes PRIMARY) in test_bot_cheat (loopback, with an honest control bot) and in test_net_processes (three processes at once): none does extra damage, suspicion rises with rewind (one per hit) and the 95 % with rtt=150 and jitter=30 is CMB-09's test.

Delivers the first per-battle player limit in Vado Gris, recorded in networking.md. Includes the combat BotBrain, the reconciliation with rtt=120 and 5 % loss, and 64 clustered bots: it sets the first per-battle player limit. Depends on: TEC-10, TEC-11, CMB-06, CMB-13, CMB-14, CMB-15. Done (H5): `load_test.gd --brawl` with the phase 4 kit (abilities, potions, knock-downs and finishers; half the fakes marksmen, pure archers with `--fake-archers` and part shooting at the sky with `--sky-archers`), the `combat_projectiles` section and the arrows in the summary. Tick and projectile p99: 32, 11.3 and 0.98 ms (met); 48, 17.0 and 0.89 ms; 64, 19.3 and 1.68 ms (not met). Per-battle player limit with the kit: 40, recorded in networking.md 14. H5 review (docs/abilities.md §11.10): the 40 came from interpolating; measured, 12.4 and 0.90 ms (met), and the 40-player skirmish enters the `--brawl` battery; it is the default capacity of the Vado Gris server (`Zones.MAX_PLAYERS`, `NetZones.max_players`; a zone without its own capacity stays at 64). The 64-arrow cap, filled by force (all archers shooting at the sky): peak 64, 4603 despawned without errors and 3.09 ms p99 of the projectiles. The fort's 20 vs 20 with the kit, which were an estimate, were measured in the review of the integration of the abilities on F6b (GUE-16): 16.2 ms p99 with the default template and 16.9 (with 1.01 of projectiles) with half marksmen, at the edge of the criterion; the limit stays at 40 (docs/networking.md 14).

- [ ] **TEC-55 Localization L1b: template, es.po maintenance and realm choice** · F5 · important · M · tools, client — In `tools/i18n`:
  - `l10n_extract.py` builds `locale/eldmark.pot`: it reads the `L10n` calls by position according to their signature (it does not mistake the keys of a parameter dictionary for text), the `# l10n` tables (with `fields=` for tables of records), the `TRANSLATORS:` comments and, only for the paths of `tools/i18n/l10n_scope.txt`, the texts of .tscn files and the fields of .tres files. It decodes escapes like GDScript and writes UTF-8, because Godot's .po reader does not understand `\u`;
  - `l10n_po.py --update` and `--check` (empty, fuzzy or obsolete entries, different placeholders or BBCode, plurals, `msgid` that looks Spanish);
  - `tests/test_l10n_catalog.gd` checks that Godot reads every entry of the template the same way as the extractor;
  - a new "Localización" step in continuous integration, required by "Suite completa".
  It debuts with the development realm choice: the texts of `realm_select.tscn` with `bind_scene`, the realm names from `RealmText` and the name and address errors with `NameRuleText` in the client (from the codes and limits of `NameRules`). Finished when continuous integration fails with an outdated template or an untranslated entry, and the captures `eleccion_de_reino_01` to `05` come out in English and in Spanish with their Spanish snapshot intact. Depends on: UI-57.
- [ ] **TEC-56 Localization L1c: scan, mechanical migration and test rewriting** · F5 · important · M · tools, client — In `tools/i18n`:

  - `l10n_migrate.py` with `extract`, `apply` and `verify`: proof by tokens that in GDScript only literals, wrappers and marks change, and by properties that in .tscn and .tres only the marked texts change. `mark` and `const` only apply to literals stored as they are; whatever is formatted before being stored goes to the hand pass;
  - `l10n_rewrite_tests.py`: turns into English the assertions of the tests that compare Spanish texts.
  `verify` alone does not prove that the Spanish does not change; it does together with the Spanish snapshot of each screen. The tools debut by migrating the title screen (sign in and forgotten password). Finished when the scan fails on an unmarked text in a migrated file, `verify` rejects a doctored change and `titulo_01_inicio` and `titulo_02_olvide_contrasena` come out in English and in Spanish. Depends on: TEC-55.
- [ ] **TEC-57 Localization L4a: network format of the server messages (protocol 8)** · F5 · important · M · shared, server, client, operations — The final network format, in one go:
  - **Catalog:** `ServerMessages`, shared and with no text. Each message has a u16 id, a stable key (`party.declined`) and typed parameters: integer, name, free text (u16, up to 768 B, because the inventory GM replies exceed 200 B today), Unix time, seconds, realm or object. The English templates live in the client. Its hash enters `compute_sim_hash`;
  - **NOTICE** carries a message block instead of text and gains the CHAT code, which replaces the CHAT_MESSAGE of the system channel;
  - **REJECT** keeps the v7 header forever. The rejections that come before the version check (MALFORMED, which goes out first, and VERSION) carry no block and their text is fixed English. The decoder accepts and discards whatever it does not understand after the header, so that a future version can extend it;
  - **Transition:** a `legacy.text {text}` id that all current senders use with today's text, so nothing changes visibly; TEC-58 to TEC-60 replace it area by area;
  - **Version:** `PROTOCOL_VERSION` becomes 8;
  - **Go platform:** regenerate `progression_rules.json` with `tools/progression/export_rules.gd` (`test_progression_rules_export` fails until then), `main_test.go` reads the protocol from `gamedata` instead of sending "7" (otherwise all its requests get 426), `platformtest.Protocol` comes from `gamedata`, `ELDMARK_MIN_PROTOCOL=8` in `staging.env.example` and `production.env.example`, and a deployment note in platform.md (new client, zones and minimum in the same maintenance window);
  - a debug command that sends a notice and a test system line, for the bench; message tests, raw ENet tests and fuzz tests brought up to date; networking.md describes protocol 8.
  Finished when the Godot suite, "Plataforma" and "Plataforma e2e" pass with protocol 8, a v7 client reads its `REJECT VERSION` and there are captures of the test notice and the system line in English and in Spanish (with the same Spanish text in both: it is the "before" of TEC-58 to TEC-60). Coordinate with TEC-52, which touches the same Go tests. Depends on: TEC-55.
